#### MED-001: desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:515 — the window opened at a fixed 1200 × 800, taller than a 1080p screen's work area at 150 % scaling
- D4's `resize(1200, 800)` is logical pixels. At 150 % display scaling a 1920 × 1080 screen's work area is about 1280 × 690 logical, so the window's bottom — Review's Save / Write / Copy / Complete / Discard row and the status bar — would open under the taskbar until the practitioner maximised it. Qt does not bound an explicit `resize` by the screen. Phase 1's smoke runs at 150 %, so this is the practitioner's real setup, not a corner case.
- Fix: `shell.opening_size(available_width, available_height)` (D4's `OPENING_SIZE` = 1200 × 800, each side bounded by the work area) and `self.resize(*opening_size(...))` from `self.screen().availableGeometry()`. The minimum 960 × 640 is unchanged (D4); a work area smaller than it is named in the smoke instead (below).
- Tests: `test_ui_shell.py::TestOpeningSize` (C14 both ways, an injected work area: two large screens keep 1200 × 800, 1080p at 150 % and 1366 × 768 at 125 % are bounded) and `TestWindowShell::test_the_tab_bar_is_hidden_and_the_window_has_its_icon_and_minimum` (the window's size is within the bound its own screen gives, whatever the host's). The Phase 3 smoke script's step 10 gains the 150 % reopen check; Task 3.3's line carries a dated note.
- Triage: Fix-now (MED, do-the-work, not production-impacting — headless AUTO-DISPOSABLE); /fix decision: Applied; /fix date: 2026-10-17

#### LOW-002: desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/theme.py:612 — `theme.colour` turned any string into a colour, a door past the D13 confinement guard
- The guard (`test_ui_theme.py::test_no_visual_value_or_setter_outside_the_theme`, review round 4) bans importing `QColor` outside `ui/theme.py` and the `#rrggbb` search catches hex values, but `theme.colour("red")` from any module would have built a named colour neither sees — exactly what the guard was widened to stop.
- Fix: `colour` accepts only a value one of `LIGHT` / `DARK` holds (`_TOKEN_COLOURS`, derived from the dataclass fields — never a list of known spellings) and raises `ValueError` otherwise. Every caller (`ui/icons.py`, fed `tokens.text_muted` / `tokens.accent` by the rail and `theme.LIGHT.*` for the app mark) already passes a token value.
- Tests: `test_ui_shell.py::TestIcons::test_only_a_token_colour_is_drawn` ("red", a stray hex, "", "transparent" refused by `colour` and by `icons.pixmap`) and `test_every_token_colour_is_accepted` (both sets, every colour field).
- Triage: Fix-now; /fix decision: Applied; /fix date: 2026-10-17

#### Review inputs — dispositions (no finding)
- **Task 3.4's deviation (`chrome_view_changed = Signal(str, str)`) — CONFORMS, and is the smallest correct shape.** D4 needs the header to withhold the live line on Past sessions; C8 forbids splitting a composed text. With one string the header could only withhold by splitting it; with the view it would hold a `ChromeView` (patient field included) and import the model's rules into the shell. Two strings, both built by `models.header_link_text(view, live=...)` from the view's fields, keep the shell holding only display text and the rule in the Qt-free model. The bridge's change is two lines; `chrome_view_text`'s output is unchanged (its link and live lines moved into two private helpers it shares; every `TestChromeView` pin passed). The Task 3.4 line records what was built and why.
- **The three `ui/theme.py` additions — CONFORM (one tightened, LOW-002).** `current_tokens()` is module state set by `apply_theme` before it emits, read only by the shell's constructors (no application call from the shell, C9). `colour()` is now token-only. ### Round 13 - 2026-10-17 - Stage-3 (Phase 3 the shell), independent cross-family codex peer review (pass stage-3.p1)
Recorded by: the composer (composer-seat codex round, read-only sandbox; transcribed verbatim from `.cursor/loops/stage-3-peer-r13.log`, the last line-start header; the six per-job evidence lines precede the block in the log)
- Round status: Closed (0 pending)
- Source: independent cross-family codex peer review
- Reviewer: codex gpt-6-astra (medium)
- Scope: Phase 3 working-tree changes against `5f5141c`, the three named new files, permitted supporting code and plan sections; read-only static review.

#### Findings

- **PR-MED-001** (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:515`): The opening-size fix bounds the client area, so the decorated window still exceeds a constrained work area. `QWidget.resize()` excludes the native title bar and frame: the tested 690-pixel work area receives a 690-pixel client height, leaving no room for those decorations even though the 640-pixel minimum would permit a fitting window. The promised whole-window fit therefore remains unmet. — Evidence: `work_area = self.screen().availableGeometry()` followed by `self.resize(*opening_size(work_area.width(), work_area.height()))`; `ui/shell.py:73` returns `min(width, available_width), min(height, available_height)`; `desktop/tests/test_ui_shell.py:197` expects `((1280, 690), (1200, 690))`, and line 227 checks only `window.width()` / `window.height()`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Account for native frame margins when bounding the opening client size, retain the minimum, and verify decorated-window containment using injected work-area and frame dimensions. /fix decision: Applied
  - `shell.opening_size(available_width, available_height, *, frame=FRAME_ALLOWANCE)` now bounds the CLIENT size by the work area minus the frame, so the decorated window fits; `FRAME_ALLOWANCE = (16, 48)` logical pixels (Windows 11's caption is about 32 at every scale, plus the frame) because Qt cannot measure the native frame before the window is shown. On 1080p at 150 % (1280 × 690) the window opens at 1200 × 642 client — the 640 minimum still fits, unshrunk. New `shell.opening_size_for(screen)` reads the screen's work area and falls back to D4's 1200 × 800 when there is no screen (the peer's noted None case); `MainWindow.__init__` calls it once, after `setMinimumSize(960, 640)`, which is unchanged. No other screen behaviour changes.
  - Tests: `test_ui_shell.py::TestOpeningSize` — six injected work-area / frame pairs, each asserting the exact client size and that client + frame fits the work area (C14 both ways: large screens keep 1200 × 800; a zero frame fills the work area; a taller frame or a 1366 × 768 screen at 125 % go below the minimum, where Qt's minimum wins); the default allowance on 1080p at 150 % keeps the minimum; a fake screen is asked for its work area; no screen opens at 1200 × 800. The window test reads its bound from `opening_size_for(window.screen())`. Task 3.3's line carries a dated note; the Phase 3 smoke's step 10 already checks the whole window, title bar included, at 150 %.
  - /fix date: 2026-10-17

- Verification counts: 3 claims checked, 1 confirmed, 2 dropped as unverifiable
- Last reviewed: 2026-10-17
- Leg-1 verification (executor, 2026-10-17T11:11:56+11:00): PR-MED-001 — CONFIRMED materiality=material severity=MED surface=desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:515 rec=Fix-now — `QWidget.resize` on a top-level window sets the client size only (Qt's frame excludes it, and before `show()` the native frame cannot be read), so Round 11's 1200 × 690 client on a 690-px work area opened a decorated window about 32 px taller than the work area — the title bar or the bottom row off-screen, the very case Round 11 meant to close; the peer's unguarded `screen()` None is folded into the same fix (fallback to D4's size).
- Round status (leg 1): Closed (0 pending).
- Cap verdict: converging — peer_round 1 of cap 5 found one MED, a same-surface tail of the executor's own Round 11 fix (now fixed with injected frame dimensions); nothing else in Phase 3's code or tests was found, and the six per-job evidence lines confirmed the rest.

Finding verification: 5 candidates; 3 dropped; 0 downgraded. Dropped: (1) "the folded summary can go stale when the transcript engine hides `generate_box` without `closed`". `_clear` without `closed` runs only from `begin_live_view` (a Start) and `clear_live_view` (a Discard during recording, `ui/transcript.py:429`, `:467`). Neither can run while a note is under review or saved: Start is refused during a review, and a queued session blocks it. `show_document` runs before the note engine shows a note on every route. No reachable path was found. (2) "the scroll area lets the splitter squeeze the right column below its content's minimum width". Before S.2 the splitter collapsed it to nothing at that point (`childrenCollapsible`); now it scrolls with the content reachable. That is not worse, and it is the practitioner's "scroll". (3) "the radio outline is half a pixel off Fusion's circle". Fusion centres it at `QRect.center() + (1, 1)`, and `IndicatorStyle` at `QRectF(rect).center()`. The two agree for an even width, and the style sheet sets every indicator to 16 × 16.

#### LOW-001: docs/design-system.md (Tokens, `indicator`) — "both card surfaces" names `surface_alt`, which is not a card surface
- The token line said the indicator border is at 3:1 against "the window and both card surfaces". The test (`test_ui_theme.py::TestIndicatorBorder`) checks `canvas`, `surface` and `surface_alt`. `surface_alt` is the palette's Base (`ui/theme.py`'s palette), the indicator's own fill, and a card is `surface` only (`mark_card`, `QFrame[card="true"]`).
- Fix: "against the window (`canvas`), the card surface (`surface`) and the indicator's own fill (`surface_alt`, the palette's Base)". Pattern siblings (searched "card surface", "card surfaces", "surface_alt" in `docs/`, `CHANGELOG.md` and the plan): the plan's S.1 as-built line names the three tokens correctly, and S.1's own task line ("window and card surfaces") is the practitioner's wording of the requirement — left. None else.
- Triage: Fix-now (LOW, docs, do-the-work — headless AUTO-DISPOSABLE); /fix decision: Applied; /fix date: 2026-10-21

#### LOW-002: CHANGELOG.md (0.4.0, Phase S) and the re-check script — an existing taskbar pin no longer joins the running window
- Since S.7 the app process declares an explicit taskbar identity (`app.py`'s `declare_taskbar_identity` before `QApplication`). A Clinic Scribe taskbar pin made before 0.4.0 copies the shortcut as it was then, with no identity. Windows associates it with the executable's path, not with `ClinicScribe.App`, so after the upgrade it shows as a second button beside the running window. Re-creating the Start-menu shortcut (`packaging/scribe.iss`'s `[Icons]`) does not change a pin already made. The reverse holds after a rollback to 0.3.3 (a pin made under 0.4.0 carries the identity; 0.3.3's process declares none). Nothing told the practitioner, and the re-check's step 8 would have read the old pin's second button as a failure.
- Fix: one CHANGELOG sentence (both directions: unpin and pin again from the Start menu), and step 8 of the "Phase S re-check script" now unpins any 0.3.x pin first. Documents only; no behaviour change. Pattern siblings (searched "Rolling back from 0.4.0", "taskbar" in `docs/release/pilot-builds.md`): that document has no 0.4.0 section yet (H5 writes it), so none.
- Triage: Fix-now (LOW, docs, do-the-work, not production-impacting — headless AUTO-DISPOSABLE); /fix decision: Applied; /fix date: 2026-10-21

#### Review inputs (the composer's six, each with its disposition)
- **S.2's folded summary row.**
  - What can reach it: only `models.generate_summary_text(role, profile)`. `role` is `TranscriptScreen._selected_role()`, the checked radio's speaker key; `profile` is `profile_combo.currentText()`, only once a profile is confirmed. Neither is the radio's text (which quotes a transcript sentence). The label is plain text with Qt's default, non-selectable interaction. `test_a_draft_folds_generate_to_one_row_of_a_label_and_a_name` checks that no transcript sentence and no quote mark appears, while the radios do quote one.
  - Unfold paths: Change (`unfold_generate`). A cleared review — `NoteScreen.clear` emits `review_changed`; Cancel review and regenerate clears, and so do Complete and Discard through `closed` and the window's clear. A new note folds again, because `_unfolded` resets whenever no note is shown.
  - Generate's behaviour is unchanged: the holder is hidden, never `generate_box` itself, so D14's `setVisible(self._can_generate)` and `_clear`'s `hide()` are untouched. No connection or enablement in `ui/transcript.py` changed — the one addition is the read-only `generation_choice()`. CONFORMS.
- **S.1's indicator style.** Contrast is pinned in both schemes through `force_scheme`: 3:1 for `indicator`, and 2:1 and weaker for `text_disabled`, against `canvas`, `surface` and `surface_alt`. Disabled reads disabled; focus takes `focus`, only under `State_KeyboardFocusChange` as Fusion's own does. Nothing else changes: `drawPrimitive` calls Fusion first for every element and adds a stroke only for the two indicator primitives, so text, enabled state, tab order and the tick or dot are Fusion's. The style sheet still gives the indicators a size only, so `QStyleSheetStyle` falls back to the base style — now `IndicatorStyle` — as it did to Fusion. Checkable group-box titles and item-view checkboxes also draw `PE_IndicatorCheckBox`, so they gain the same outline: consistent with "every indicator" and recorded. C9/C10: the class lives in `ui/theme.py` and is set only by `apply_theme`'s first apply. It paints with Qt's painter — no file asset, no QSS `image:`, no QtSvg. The package's AST confinement of `QColor` / `QPen` / `QPainter` admits `ui/theme.py` only, and the composer's run kept `test_ui_theme.py`'s guard green. CONFORMS.
- **S.7's taskbar seam.** No in-process test calls Windows: the conftest's autouse `taskbar_ids` replaces `taskbar.set_process_app_id` for every test. The `main()`-driven tests record through it; S.7's own tests assert the recorded id, and that a raising seam returns False without raising. The suite's child processes (`test_integration_no_sockets.py`'s three app children, `test_status_and_app.py`'s holder child) build their own `QApplication` and never call `main()` or `declare_taskbar_identity` (grep). Production identities are unchanged (installation plan C2): two new constants, no existing value touched, `test_identity.py`'s production pins unchanged. The dev and production ids differ (`ClinicScribe.App` / `ClinicScribe.App.Dev`), asserted by value. The `.iss` shortcut's `AppUserModelID` is `ClinicScribe.App`, equal to production's (`test_installer_script.py`). The call sits after `app_start` and before `QApplication([])`, so before any window — the order is asserted for both channels. CONFORMS.
- **S.6.** Every pause cue enters through `_show_pause_cue`: `pause_for` without a bridge, and `bridge.pause_cue` with one (`ui/main_window.py:858`) — the hotkey, sleep, lock and Chrome. Every exit from PAUSED is seen by `_on_state_shown` — the button, Chrome's Resume (through the Record page's slot), the hotkey (`on_hotkey` → `session_screen.on_resume`), Finish and Discard. Both go through ONE helper, `_clear_pause_cue`, which clears only while the cue is still the current message. So the hotkey's `HOTKEY_RESUMED_STATUS`, an expiry warning and every other `showMessage` (fourteen sites, unchanged) stay. No status-bar text changed. CONFORMS.
- **`test_shadow_exits.py`.** Unchanged (`git diff 460e836` shows nothing for it) and passing in the composer's run. No construction moved: `ui/note.py` gains a signal and three `emit` lines, and `ui/transcript.py` a read method and a comment. The new label, button, holder and scroll area are built in `ui/review.py`, and no new text widget is in `ui/note.py` or `ui/past_sessions.py`. CONFORMS.
- **The documents.** The design system's rail, status-bar, C6-exception, Review-screen, pause-cue and token paragraphs and the CHANGELOG's Phase S paragraph were each read against the code. Every claim is backed except LOW-001's wording and LOW-002's omission. #### Review inputs (each with its disposition)
- **S.5's cause and fix — FIRST INPUT.** No production code changed in this leg. The cause is not established, and the S.5 line now records why reading cannot settle it. Since Qt 6.8 the click becomes a `QContextMenuEvent` only if the press or release (by the platform's trigger) went unaccepted; the package has nothing on the path that accepts or filters one; and leg u7x1's route skipped Qt's platform handling. So the one change is to the test, which becomes evidence: the real platform route beside the old one, under both triggers, with every delivery logged. It therefore does NOT change the copy surface: `_place_note_text` and its callers, `_copy_allowed`, `copy_selection`, `build_context_menu`'s enabled states, the shadow refusals and every text-interaction flag are untouched (no file under `desktop/src` changed in this leg). `test_shadow_exits.py` is unchanged (`git diff 460e836` shows nothing for it). The S.5 gate (a cause in 0.3.3 whose fix would touch the copy surface) cannot be judged until the cause is known; the line says which run result leads where. CONFORMS (evidence leg; no fix on a guess).
- **S.4's assertion soundness.** The composer's question, answered from the code. A `QToolButton`'s size hint for text under the icon is the wider of icon and text, plus two spaces' advance, plus the style-sheet box and the style's margins: a preference, not a floor, and a min = max rail need not meet it. Elision is decided per line against the label's rectangle (the button less the rule's border and padding), so that is what the test now pins, with 15 % for 150 % rounding and a message naming the button, line and widths. The rail-width pin and the caption, accessible-name and tooltip pins are unchanged. CONFORMS.
- **Test isolation.** The new test restores the trigger and removes the filter in `finally`. It still builds and closes its own window, and the class's watchdog and `_MenuSpy` apply to all four cases, so no real popup can block. `QtTest` is imported only inside the test (the package imports nothing new). CONFORMS.

### Round 34 - 2026-10-21 - Stage-7 (Phase S smoke fixes, slice B tests), independent cross-family codex peer review (pass stage-7.p1)
composer (transcribed from the codex log, 2026-10-21)

- Round status: Closed (0 pending) — /fix 2026-10-21, stage-7 leg u7x5: all three applied
- Source: independent cross-family codex peer review
- Reviewer: codex gpt-6-astra (medium)
- Scope: Phase S test changes against `460e836`, complete `test_ui_phase_s.py`, and targeted production/plan verification; static inspection only.

#### Findings

- **PR-MED-001** (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/conftest.py:288`): The taskbar fake does not prevent accidental calls to the real Windows implementation. Direct calls or aliases bypassing the public seam can change the test process’s taskbar identity. — Evidence: the fixture only executes `monkeypatch.setattr(taskbar, "set_process_app_id", declared.append)`; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/taskbar.py:30` remains callable and executes `ctypes.WinDLL("shell32", use_last_error=True)`. The test at `desktop/tests/test_ui_phase_s.py:717` checks that the seam differs from the real function, but never verifies that reaching the real implementation fails safely. Recommendation: Fix-now — Add a fail-closed guard below the public seam and verify it without invoking Windows; ensure the wrapper’s broad exception handler cannot swallow the test failure. /fix decision: Applied; /fix notes: verified first — the conftest replaced only `taskbar.set_process_app_id`; `_win32_set_process_app_id` stayed reachable by a direct call or any name bound to it at import, and `declare_taskbar_identity`'s `except Exception` would swallow an `AssertionError` raised there. Fixed in `desktop/tests/conftest.py` only (no production change: `taskbar.py` looks `ctypes.WinDLL` up at call time). The new autouse `taskbar_guard` fixture puts `TaskbarGuard` in place of `taskbar.ctypes`, so every route below the seam records the reach and raises, never loading shell32. The fixture fails the test at teardown when anything was recorded, so the wrapper cannot swallow the failure — C6's seam rule, as with `Win32WindowsLayer` and `PartKernel`. `taskbar_ids` is unchanged. Verified by the new `test_ui_phase_s.py::test_the_real_call_below_the_seam_fails_closed_in_tests`: a direct call raises the guard's error, and the seam pointed back at the real function returns False through `declare_taskbar_identity`. The guard records both (`["shell32", "shell32"]`), and the test clears the record so it does not fail itself. ruff and mypy clean; pytest owed to the composer. /fix date: 2026-10-21; /fix applied by: Claude Code

- **PR-LOW-002** (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_ui_phase_s.py:142`): Several new tests clean up their windows only after all assertions succeed. A regression therefore skips cleanup and leaves windows, signal connections and potentially workers alive in the shared application, contaminating subsequent tests. — Evidence: the window is created at line 113, assertions run at lines 117–141, and `_close(window)` runs only at line 142 without `finally`. The same pattern occurs throughout `TestGenerateFold`, the structural right-column test, both viewport-menu tests and `TestPauseCueClears`; the platform-menu test also starts its cleanup protection only after generation and layout. Recommendation: Fix-now — Register cleanup immediately after construction through a fixture/finalizer or wrap the complete window lifetime in `try/finally`, retaining worker-aware shutdown. /fix decision: Applied; /fix notes: verified first — every window the module built (via `_window` and `_main_window`) was closed only after its assertions: of the 17 test functions that build one, 14 had no `finally` at all (`TestGenerateFold`'s five, the structural right-column test, both viewport-menu tests, `TestPauseCueClears`'s six), and in the other three — the shown right-column test, the Past-sessions transcript test and the platform-menu test — the `try` began only after setup. Fixed in `desktop/tests/test_ui_phase_s.py` only: the module's own `_window` / `_main_window` wrap the imported builders (`_review_window`, `_screens_main_window`) and register each window in `_open_windows` the moment it exists; `_close` keeps its worker-aware shutdown (`QThread.wait`, `close`, `deleteLater`) and is now once-only (it closes only a registered window, unregistering it); a new autouse `_windows_closed` fixture closes, after every test, whatever window is still registered — so a failed assertion anywhere after construction leaves nothing alive. The tests' own `_close` calls stay (they now unregister). ruff clean; pytest owed. /fix date: 2026-10-21; /fix applied by: Claude Code

- **PR-LOW-003** (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_ui_theme.py:695`): The new contrast test restores the forced scheme and application marker but leaves the global current tokens changed. With an initially dark application—or an assertion failure during the dark iteration—later painting can use tokens inconsistent with the application’s actual palette and stylesheet. — Evidence: line 684 calls `theme.apply_theme(qapp)`, which assigns `_current = tokens` at `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/theme.py:742`; cleanup only calls `theme.force_scheme(None)` and `qapp.setProperty(theme.SCHEME_PROPERTY, saved)`. `_FakeAppSurface` patches application setters, not `_current`, and `ensure_applied` skips reapplication when that restored marker is present (`ui/theme.py:789`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Save and restore the token state with monkeypatch or guaranteed cleanup, without restyling the shared application. /fix decision: Applied; /fix notes: verified first — `apply_theme` assigns the module's `_current` (`ui/theme.py`), and the test restored only `force_scheme(None)` and the marker. Pattern siblings (searched `apply_theme(` in `desktop/tests`): `test_ui_theme.py`'s Fusion test (`apply_theme(qapp, "light")`, twice) and `test_force_scheme_switches_light_dark_light` (ends on light) leave `_current` at LIGHT whatever the shared application's scheme. `test_a_repeat_of_the_same_scheme_restyles_nothing` applies the application's own scheme, so it is consistent and left. Fixed in all three: `monkeypatch.setattr(theme, "_current", theme._current)` before the first apply, so pytest puts the token set back on every path — a failed assertion included — without restyling anything. ruff clean; pytest owed. /fix date: 2026-10-21; /fix applied by: Claude Code

The filter object is the content's ancestor, so the content is deleted inside the area's base-class destructor, when the binding's override is no longer the dynamic target, and Qt drops a destroyed filter. (2) "re-asking the splitter on every content re-layout resets a handle the practitioner dragged". `QSplitter` re-shares from the sizes it stored at the drag, and before S.2 the right pane sat in the splitter and asked it on every re-layout just the same. (3) "`_WrappingLine`'s one-line `sizeHint` makes a column wider than before". A size hint is a preference, never a minimum, and it is the width these labels asked for before this leg, unwrapped.

- [x] 🟩 `[done 2026-10-21 — desktop GREEN 8095 passed / 9 skipped, ruff and mypy clean (71 files); the practitioner's live re-check PASS]` **S.1 (F2, HIGH) Empty tick boxes and radio buttons visible in both schemes.** In dark (and at 150 % the worst), an UNTICKED `QCheckBox` / unselected `QRadioButton` shows no outline — Fusion draws the indicator frame darker than the window, so on the dark window it vanishes; the label reads as plain text and the clinician cannot see that a consent tick exists. A ticked box is clear. Seen: Record's two consent ticks, Practitioner → Consent, Settings → Status (Shadow mode, Keep recordings, Allow Cliniko writes), the Review screen's clinician radios and "Show flagged lines only", Past sessions' "Hide names", Writing style's radios.
  - Fix: the theme owns it (`ui/theme.py`, C9/D1) — every indicator gets a visible border from a token with contrast against both the window and card surfaces, light and dark, a disabled variant that still reads disabled, and the checked state keeps its mark. No file asset and no QtSvg (C10): a `QProxyStyle` (or equivalent painter route) owned by the theme, not a QSS `image:`. Nothing else about a checkbox changes (text, enabled state, focus, tab order).
  - Done when: a test pins, for both schemes through `force_scheme`, that the indicator border token differs from the window and card colours by a stated contrast; and the live re-check shows every box and circle above visible when empty.
  - As built (leg u7x1): a new token role `indicator` (light `#6b7280`, dark `#9ca3af`) and `theme.IndicatorStyle(QProxyStyle)` over Fusion, set once by `apply_theme`'s first apply (the module's `_style`). Its `drawPrimitive` lets Fusion draw `PE_IndicatorCheckBox` / `PE_IndicatorRadioButton` as before (so the tick and the dot are Fusion's own) and then strokes a 1-px outline over the frame — a rectangle for a box, a circle for a radio — in `indicator`, `text_disabled` when disabled, `focus` when it has keyboard focus. The colour is read from `current_tokens()` at paint time, so a live scheme change needs no restyle. No file asset, no QSS `image:`, no QtSvg; nothing else about a checkbox changes. Tests (`test_ui_theme.py::TestIndicatorBorder`): contrast ≥ 3:1 against `canvas`, `surface` and `surface_alt` in both schemes through `force_scheme`; the disabled outline ≥ 2:1 and weaker than the enabled one; the focus colour; a pixel on the drawn outline of a box and a radio, light and dark, enabled and disabled. Named changes: the designed-colour-role set and the light/dark-differ list gain `indicator`; the Fusion test asserts the app's style is an `IndicatorStyle` whose base style is Fusion.

Practitioner's choice 2026-10-20: two lines — "Past" over "sessions" under the clock icon; the rail keeps its width; the button's accessible name and tooltip stay "Past sessions"; the other labels unchanged.
  - Done when: a test pins the two-line caption and the unchanged accessible name; the caption is not elided at the rail width at 100 % and 150 %.
  - As built (leg u7x1): `ui/shell.py` gains `RAIL_CAPTIONS = {"Past sessions": "Past\nsessions"}` and `rail_caption(name)`; the rail button's text is the caption, its accessible name the page name, and a tooltip the page name where they differ. The rail width token is unchanged. Tests: `test_ui_shell.py::test_past_sessions_reads_on_two_lines_and_no_caption_is_cut` (text, accessible name and tooltip; the rail's width fixed; every rail button at least as wide as its size hint; Past sessions' widest line, with a 15 % allowance, fits). `[leg u7x2]` The composer's run failed it at `rail.width() == rail_width` (85 against 84): a test expectation, not a layout change. `QFrame#rail`'s `min-width` and `max-width` are both the token (`ui/theme.py`'s rail rule), and Qt's style-sheet box model applies them as CONTENT widths, adding the rule's border — its one 1-px `border-right` — so the rail has been fixed at token + 1 since Phase 3, whatever a caption's size hint asks (min = max, so no caption can widen it). The test now pins `minimumWidth() == maximumWidth() == width() == rail_width + 1`, with that reason beside it. `[leg u7x3]` The composer's next run failed the same test further down, at `button.width() >= button.sizeHint().width()` for "Record" (76 against 109). That assertion was unsound. `QToolButton::sizeHint` for text under the icon is the wider of the icon and the text PLUS two spaces' width, then the style-sheet box (border and padding) and the style's margins. It is a preference that a fixed-width rail (min = max) need not meet, and it is not what decides whether Qt cuts a caption. What decides is Qt's tool-button label code: it splits the caption at `\n` and elides a LINE (ElideMiddle — the smoke's "Past ...sions") only when that line is wider than the label's rectangle, the button less its style-sheet border and padding. The test now pins exactly that: for every rail button, each caption line's `horizontalAdvance` × 1.15 (150 % rounding) ≤ the button's width − 2 × (1-px border + `space_1` padding, `QToolButton#railButton`'s rule). The message names the button, the line and both widths. One risk is named: offscreen text metrics come from the offscreen font, not Segoe UI, so a failure there needs the message's numbers read before any caption changes. The live re-check (step 9) stays the judge. `[leg u7x4]` It did fail offscreen — on "Record" (72 px against a 66-px label), which the practitioner's display renders uncut — so an absolute pixel check pins the test platform's font, not what the practitioner sees. The test is now FONT-INDEPENDENT. It pins the caption split, the accessible name and tooltip, the rail's fixed width and every rail button's equal width. In the button's own font metrics (whatever the platform's font), each line of "Past sessions" is no wider than the widest one-line caption ("Record", "Review", "Recovery", "Settings" — the ones the live display never cut), and the one-line name was wider: the split brings the caption to a width the rail already shows whole. Pixel fit at 100 % and 150 % is the live re-check's (step 9) alone. Named change: `test_the_rail_has_the_four_everyday_entries_and_the_gear` reads the texts as `Past\nsessions` and the accessible names as the four titles. 150 % is the live re-check's (offscreen has no scale factor).

Nothing in the package installs an event filter, sets a context-menu policy, overrides a mouse handler, grabs the mouse or swallows the event in `nativeEvent`; Qt's route (the viewport's event → `viewportEvent` → `QFrame::event` → `_NotePanel.contextMenuEvent`) reads correct inside the adopted layout and the new scroll area, and no exception path was found in `build_context_menu`. Whether it is pre-existing in 0.3.3 is not established. Two event-path tests in `test_ui_phase_s.py::TestNoteBodyMenu` (`QMenu.exec` faked): (1) a real `QContextMenuEvent` sent to the note body's viewport opens the menu, Copy disabled before Save and enabled after it, refused for shadow; (2) a right-button press and release sent to the window's `QWindow` at the viewport's visible centre (asserting `childAt` is the viewport) opens it — Qt's own mouse-to-context-menu path. Reading the composer's result: if (1) passes and (2) fails, the cause is in that synthesis and is fixed in the resumed leg; if both pass, the live failure is platform-only and S.5 becomes a must-pause question for the practitioner's re-check (step 6 of the re-check script). `[leg u7x2]` THE HANG: the composer's run blocked for over two hours on test (1) (the nine tests before it passed). The spy had replaced `exec` on PySide's `QMenu` CLASS, and the panel's call `menu.exec(...)` evidently did not reach it, so a real popup's nested event loop ran offscreen with nothing to close it. (The likely reason, not proven: `QMenu.exec` has static and instance overloads, and Shiboken resolves such a name on the instance before the class dictionary.) Fixed in the TEST only: `_MenuSpy` now wraps the body's `build_context_menu` on the INSTANCE — the pattern `test_ui_screens.py`'s context-menu test already uses. The panel's own builder still builds the real menu, its entries are recorded, and `contextMenuEvent` gets a stand-in whose `exec` opens nothing. An autouse watchdog in `TestNoteBodyMenu` closes any real popup on a 100 ms timer and fails the test, so a regression can never block the suite again. Neither test needs a real popup: the menu's contents are built before `exec`, and Qt's mouse-to-context-menu synthesis — test (2)'s subject — happens before the panel's handler runs. So the discriminator stays in the suite. The live re-check (step 6) stays the judge of the real popup. `[leg u7x3]` The composer's run: the hang gone; both viewport tests PASS; test (2) FAILS — no menu (`spy.opened == []`), with every assertion before it passing. CORRECTION of leg u7x2's reading: that failure does not yet show the live bug reproduced offscreen. Test (2) sent the press and release to the `QWindow` with `QApplication.sendEvent`, which skips Qt's platform mouse handling. And since Qt 6.8 (the build lock has PySide6 6.11.1) a click becomes a `QContextMenuEvent` only when the press — or the release, by the platform's `QStyleHints.contextMenuTrigger`, which is Release on Windows and Press offscreen — was not accepted on its way up the widget chain. The package has no mouse handler, context-menu policy, event filter on this path or `WA_NoMousePropagation`. The `_NotePanel` (a `QPlainTextEdit`) leaves its text control to ignore a right press. So the cause cannot be pinned by reading, and this leg makes NO production change on a guess. Test (2) becomes the evidence instead, `test_a_right_button_click_on_the_shown_window_opens_the_menu[route × trigger]`, four cases. `platform_input` is a REAL input event, `QTest.mouseClick` on the `QWindow`, through Qt's platform handling as the practitioner's mouse goes. `window_event` is the old `sendEvent` route. Each runs under the `press` and `release` triggers, forced through `QStyleHints.setContextMenuTrigger` and restored after. An application-wide `_EventLog` filter records, in order, every press, release and context-menu delivery and its receiver, and a failure prints that list. The list shows how far each press or release climbed (where it stopped is where it was accepted), and whether any context-menu event was made and where it went. Reading the next run: `platform_input` passing under both triggers means offscreen does not reproduce the live failure, and S.5 goes to the practitioner's re-check (step 6) as a must-pause. `platform_input|release` failing while `|press` passes reproduces Windows' release trigger, and the log names the widget that accepted the release; the fix then goes on the event path (most likely the right-button release left unaccepted by the panel), proven by that case. `[2026-10-21 re-check, step 6]` PASS at 100 % and 150 %: the menu shows under the pointer, Copy and Select All greyed before Save, Copy enabled after Save on a normal recording. S.5 is CLOSED live with no production change (the smoke's F6 not reproduced on this build; leg u7x4's option (a) stands).

  - Done when: a test (offscreen, a real layout after `show()`, the real splitter), failing with the fix reverted, pins at a window standing in for 150 % on 1920×1200 — before Generate with and without the next-patient line, with a note and review warnings (folded) and unfolded by Change — that the right scroll area needs no horizontal bar and the consent banner is no wider than its viewport, comparing widths with each other only; and the live re-check (step 4a) shows the column whole in those states.
  - As built (leg u7x6): THE CAUSE, by reading (two widgets, one on each side). (1) The transcript pane's legend ("Uncertain words are shown as [word?] - verify them. A line marked [possibly not speech] may be music or noise, not speech.") was a non-wrapping label, so the transcript side's minimum was that whole line — about 750 px at the theme's 10 pt — and at 150 % the splitter gave the transcript side that much. (2) S.2's `QScrollArea` asked the splitter for almost no width, so the right column got only what was left (about 420 px), while its content's minimum is set by the clinician radios — a `QRadioButton` never wraps, and each quotes up to 90 characters of its speaker's first line (`models.speaker_quotations`) — with the Generate group's two fixed lines ("Confirm the clinician and template before generating a note:", "Clinician (choose the speaker who is the clinician):"), also non-wrapping. The content was laid out at its minimum, wider than the viewport, so the word-wrapped consent paragraph wrapped at that wider width and was cut, and the stretched combo boxes ran to it. (The combo boxes ran past the edge because they stretch to the content's width; nothing here reads them as the widest item — a Template profile or Prefill region name is far shorter than a radio's quote.) Before S.2 the right pane sat in the splitter itself, which honoured its minimum. THE FIX: `ui/review.py` `_ColumnScroll(QScrollArea)` — `right_scroll` asks for its content's minimum width plus its frame and a vertical scroll bar's width (so the content fits across whether or not the column scrolls down), and re-asks the splitter whenever the content lays itself out again (its `eventFilter` on the content's `LayoutRequest` calls `updateGeometry`; the filter is the one `setWidget` installs) — the radios filled in, the group folded or unfolded by Change. Its height asks for nothing more, so S.2's vertical scrolling, the note's twelve-line minimum and stretch, the fold and Change are unchanged; the horizontal bar stays "as needed", shown only if a window too narrow for both sides' minimums ever forced it (an honest fallback, never a hidden cut). And the content now narrows where it can: in `ui/transcript.py` the legend and the Generate group's two fixed lines are a new `_WrappingLine(QLabel)` — word-wrapped, so its minimum is its widest word, but still ASKING for its one-line size (`sizeHint`: the line's `fontMetrics().size` plus the label's margins). That second half matters: `QSplitter` shares its width out in proportion to what each side asks for (its children's `sizeHint`s, both stretch factors being 1), and the transcript side asks for its legend's width; a plain word-wrapped `QLabel` asks for a golden-ratio box far narrower than the line, which would have moved the handle at EVERY scale, 100 % included (step 3's PASS look). Construction stays where it was (`test_shadow_exits.py` unchanged — it counts no `QLabel`). So the transcript side's minimum is now its widest word and its transcript box, what each side asks for is unchanged, and the splitter keeps both minimums. At 100 % on 1920×1200 nothing changes unless a radio is wider than the column's share (which at 100 % would have shown a sideways bar before); at 150 % the share is as before whenever the radios fit it, and otherwise the handle moves left just enough for the widest radio (the transcript side keeps its minimum). NAMED LIMIT: a clinician radio still does not narrow below its quote (a radio cannot wrap; eliding the quote would cut it), so on a window narrower than the transcript side's minimum plus the widest radio — well under 150 % on 1920×1200 (the test's margin is some forty characters), but the 960-px minimum window with a long quote can reach it — the splitter cannot give both sides their minimum: the column's sideways bar (kept "as needed" for this) can come back, and the transcript side may be narrowed below its own minimum too. Not measured; a wrapping clinician radio would be its own change (it would alter what the radio shows). `docs/design-system.md`'s Review-screen paragraph gains one sentence. Test: `test_ui_phase_s.py::TestRightColumnWidth::test_the_column_fits_across_at_150_percent[before-generate|next-patient|note-folded|note-unfolded]` on a shown window standing in for 1280×770 logical px — sized in the test font's own units, 180 average characters by 42 lines (about 1280×770 in the app's 10 pt Segoe UI), because the offscreen font is about one and a half times wider than Segoe UI (the S.4 run's "Record") and a fixed pixel size would stand in for a far smaller screen — over a transcript whose first line is longer than the 90-character quote (and, but for the first state, the next-patient turns), with "Change" pressed on the SHOWN window in the last state (so the live re-ask is what is tested): the right pane no wider than the viewport, the horizontal bar's range 0, the consent label shown and no wider than the viewport, each side of the splitter at least its own minimum, the legend at least its height for its width. With the fix reverted, the three states that show the radios fail (the radio is wider than the column the splitter gave); the folded state holds the fix with a note shown. And `::test_the_lines_that_now_wrap_still_ask_for_one_line`: each of the three lines wraps, asks for at least its one-line width, and has a smaller minimum (it fails for a plain wrapped label and for an unwrapped one). No pixel count is compared.

Later lines were left out - … then remove them from the recording on the Review screen." showed its fourth wrapped line half and the rest of the sentence not at all; its Acknowledge button was whole. The handle had also moved right compared with (a). The S.2 / F5 failure class (every review warning's text whole), seen once S.9 let the column take its own width. Secondary: "Edit the note"'s section box read "Presenting complai".
  - Fix: find why the row's height does not follow its width, and why the handle moved; every review warning row shows its whole text at every width the column can take (150 % and 100 %, folded and unfolded, any mix of warning kinds); the note keeps S.2's minimum height and stretch.
  - Done when: a test through the real layout, failing with the fix reverted, pins at the 150 % stand-in with a note carrying the next-patient, number and unsure warnings that each warning label is at least its height for its width and ends inside its row (font-independent, every window closed); and re-check step 4a's (b) and (c) show each warning's text whole to its last word.
  - As built (leg u7x7): THE CAUSE, by reading — not S.9's width, but S.2's height path meeting the note engine's own splitter. The note engine keeps its review side (`note_side`: the counts, the consent paragraph, the warning rows, the note, its two buttons) inside a `QSplitter` beside its own transcript pane, which the Review screen hides (D6). A `QSplitter` passes no height-for-width through (it has no layout, and its size policy declares none), so once S.2 put the column in a resizable scroll area — whose content takes, when it scrolls, the height its layout reports FOR ITS WIDTH — the engine counted at its PREFERRED height. A wrapped `QLabel` prefers a box of its own choosing (by Qt's label-sizing rule as read, not measured here: about 80 average characters wide for a long text, half that for a medium one), so the engine's preferred height is right only when the real column is about that wide. When the rows need more lines at the column's real width than at their preferred widths altogether, the engine's own layout has less height than its rows' heights for their width and squeezes them — the practitioner's cut next-patient row (most likely the one that grows: its text is long enough to keep the wide box). At 100 % the column is wide, so the rows most likely need no more lines than preferred — consistent with S.2's re-check passing there. THE FIX: `ui/review.py` lifts the engine's review side, whole, out of the engine's splitter into the engine's own layout (`_adopt(note_screen.review_side, …, 1)`, once, in `__init__`, C15) and hides the splitter, now holding only the hidden duplicate pane. The height-for-width chain then runs unbroken from every warning label to `_ColumnScroll`, so the column is as tall as its rows need at its real width. Same widget, same owner (the note engine), same place on screen; no construction moved; `note_side` never split; `test_shadow_exits.py` unchanged. `ui/note.py` gains one attribute, `self.review_side = note_side`, so the Review screen names it rather than reaching for the splitter's second child. THE HANDLE: moving right between (a) and (b) is S.9's design, kept. The column asks for its content's minimum width, and that minimum is the clinician radios' only while they show: at (a) a long quote moved the handle left just enough for the radio; at (b) the group is folded, the radios are gone, and the splitter shares the width out as it always has (S.9's as-built: "the share is as before whenever the radios fit it"). The other choice — keeping the handle where a radio once pushed it — would make the split depend on what was shown before and never come back without a drag; not chosen. THE SECTION BOX: Qt's ordinary combo clipping, not a width S.9 caused. "Edit the note"'s first row shares its width between the line chooser and the section box (both stretch 1) and "Add line to section"; a `QComboBox` never elides or wraps its shown text (Fusion clips it at the box's edge), and its minimum width is not its text (it is worked out before the items are added). Before S.9 that row sat at the content's minimum width, narrower still at 150 %; S.9 widened it. The open list shows the names. Not changed (a different width rule would change what the practitioner sees beyond F9). Test: `test_ui_phase_s.py::TestWarningRowsWhole::test_every_warning_row_shows_its_whole_text[150|100 × folded|unfolded]`. On a shown window at the stand-in in the test font's characters and lines — 180 × 42 (150 %) or 274 × 64 (100 %) — the transcript arrives first and the note is delivered on the SHOWN window, as Generate delivers it, then "Change" for the unfolded cases. The note carries `possible_next_patient`, `number_source` and `hedged_source` (asserted, the codes printed if not), from a patient's hedged line, a clinician's line with a number and a closing then a greeting. The test checks that the note engine has height-for-width, that every review row is shown and its label at least its height for its width with its bottom inside its row, and that the note body keeps its minimum. With the fix reverted `note.hasHeightForWidth()` fails in every case (a splitter declares none); the row checks fail where the rows need more lines than preferred altogether, as on the practitioner's screen, which offscreen widths need not reproduce — hence the structural check.

Practitioner decision 2026-10-21: fix now. Each row shows its line's text in full, the row's controls go on their own line under the text when the column is narrow, and the list has no sideways scroll bar (at wide widths the one-line layout may stay if it already shows the whole text — the executor's call, recorded here).

  - As built (leg u7x8): THE CAUSE, by reading. The rows are not a list widget. `NoteScreen._rebuild_edit_controls` builds one `QWidget` per line in `_lines_box`, a `QVBoxLayout` on a content widget that sat in its own `QScrollArea` (`lines_scroll`, resizable) inside the group. Two things squeezed a row:
    - (1) Sideways: each row was ONE `QHBoxLayout`, the word-wrapped text (stretch 1) beside Remove line, the section box, Move and Edit. A row's minimum width was therefore its text's widest word plus every control. That was wider than the list at 150 %, so the list scrolled sideways and the text shrank to its widest word, one word per line ("[check:" over "number]").
    - (2) Down: the list's own scroll box asked the group for its preferred height. By Qt's scroll-area sizing as read (not measured here), a scroll area's preferred size is worked out once, from its content as first measured, here an empty list. S.2 also gave "Edit the note" no stretch. So the box was short whatever its rows needed, and a row's lower lines sat below its foot — the 100 % cut.
  - THE FIX, in `ui/note.py`, building nothing new:
    - (a) Every row is the new layout helper `_text_over_controls(row, text)`: a `QVBoxLayout` with the line's text — PLAIN TEXT, word-wrapped, the row's full width — then a `QHBoxLayout` of its controls under it, ended by a stretch. The routed, added, replaced and removed rows, the pre-filled rows and the typed rows all use it. The controls are added in the same order and stay the row's DIRECT children (C5: a sub-layout makes no widget); the open editor row stays the one-line field, Apply and Cancel, and the proposal editor's heading now sits over its field. This is the layout the "Proposed additions" rows already use (heading and text over an actions row). The one-line layout is NOT kept at wide widths (the executor's call): one rule at every width means a row never jumps between layouts as the window or the handle moves, and Qt has no layout that moves controls under the text only when narrow — that would be a new layout class with its own height-for-width.
    - (b) The rows' content widget sits in the group directly; `lines_scroll` is gone (one construction fewer; no test reads it). The rows' minimum width and wrapped heights now reach the Review column. The column scrolls down (S.2), asks the splitter for its content's minimum width (S.9) and takes its height from its content's height-for-width (S.10). So a row is never cut at a short box's foot, and nothing above the rows scrolls sideways. A long note makes "Edit the note" longer, and the column scrolls; the list has no scroll of its own.
    - No construction moved, no text widget, list, item view or combo box added; `test_shadow_exits.py` unchanged.
  - NAMED, NOT CHANGED: the "Proposed additions" panel (`proposals_scroll`) is the same kind of scroll box of its own in the note side. Its rows already lay text over actions, so the sideways squeeze cannot happen there; the short-box cut could. It was not seen failing and is not F10. Recorded for the practitioner's look (step 4a (d)) and as a possible follow-up.
  - Documents: `docs/design-system.md`'s "Edits over whole lines" bullet and the CHANGELOG's Phase S paragraph gain the row layout.
